Equality is structural across all four fields via the\n28\tdataclass-generated ``__eq__``.\n29\t\n30\t1. ``eval_id``         — the FR-SCH2-validated eval identifier. The id\n31\t   flows directly into the per-eval HOME by :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`\n32\t   so it MUST satisfy the FR-SCH2 regex before any filesystem write. The\n33\t   ``__post_init__`` hook re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05) so\n34\t   a ``HomeIsolation`` can never be constructed with an unsafe id even\n35\t   when a caller bypasses the loader.\n36\t2. ``home_root``       — the scratch root directory under which the\n37\t   per-eval HOME is ``mkdtemp``-ed by :meth:`setup`. Full FR-ISO2 path\n38\t   containment lands in T02.08; this module performs only the minimal\n39\t   ``parents=True, exist_ok=True`` materialization needed to create\n40\t   sibling per-eval HOMEs concurrently.\n41\t3. ``session_id``      — the value :meth:`env` stamps into\n42\t   ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID``. Allocation lives in the orchestrator (FR-G2 /\n43\t   T03.16); this record only holds the assigned value.\n44\t4. ``time_offset_sec`` — optional offset in whole seconds for the eval's\n45\t   simulated wall clock. Default ``0`` matches DM-006 verbatim; when the\n46\t   value is non-zero :meth:`env` adds ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET``. Whether\n47\t   the subprocess actually honors the variable is gated on OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8\n48\t   / T06.03); keeping the offset opt-in via non-zero values means the\n49\t   env dict stays minimal until OQ-8 resolves.\n50\t\n51\tCOMP-006 method surface (T02.07) — FR-ISO1\n52\t==========================================\n53\t\n54\tThe four methods below are the externally observable surface of\n55\tCOMP-006. They mutate process-local filesystem state (and an internal\n56\tprivate slot tracking the created HOME path) without ever mutating the\n57\tfour DM-006 fields:\n58\t\n59\t* :meth:`setup`                 — ``mkdtemp`` under ``home_root`` and stash\n60\t  the created path on a private ``_home_path`` slot via\n61\t  ``object.__setattr__``. Returns the created path. Idempotency is one-\n62\t  shot per instance: calling :meth:`setup` twice raises ``RuntimeError``\n63\t  rather than silently leaking a directory.\n64\t* :meth:`env`                   — returns the env-var dict the subprocess\n65\t  needs: ``HOME`` always, ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` always, and\n66\t  ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when ``time_offset_sec != 0``. The\n67\t  return type pins ``dict[str, str]`` so callers can ``.update()`` it\n68\t  alongside ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` without coercion.\n69\t* :meth:`teardown`              — removes the per-eval HOME when ``keep``\n70\t  is false, leaves it on disk when ``keep`` is true (so post-mortem\n71\t  inspection or NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` tagging in T02.13 can still\n72\t  pick the directory up). Always clears the private path slot so the\n73\t  instance is safe to garbage-collect.\n74\t* :meth:`state_path`            — joins ``suffix`` under the active\n75\t  per-eval HOME so consumers (hook adapter T02.14, atomic setup T02.13,\n76\t  artifact writers) never need to import :mod:`os.path` themselves. The\n77\t  full containment check on ``suffix`` lands in T02.08; this method\n78\t  performs the minimum necessary check that the joined path stays under\n79\t  ``home_path``.\n80\t\n81\tCross-links\n82\t===========\n83\t\n84\t* DM-006 record spec (roadmap row 26).\n85\t* COMP-006 method surface (roadmap rows 28 / 32; T02.07 / T02.11).\n86\t* FR-SCH2 :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n87\t* FR-ISO2 ``containment_guard`` (T02.08) — strengthens the symlink /\n88\t  allowlist / regex checks layered on top of :meth:`setup` and\n89\t  :meth:`state_path`.\n90\t* DOC-OQ8 (T06.03) — gates ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` semantics.\n91\t* COMP-012 probe (T02.05 — ``test_isolation_layers_probe.py``) — pins the\n92\t  upstream ``IsolationLayers`` shape this module sits beside.\n93\t\"\"\"\n94\t\n95\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n96\t\n97\timport shutil\n98\timport tempfile\n99\timport traceback\n100\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n101\tfrom pathlib import Path\n102\t\n103\tfrom .config import EvalConfig, ScratchRootViolation, resolve_scratch_root\n104\tfrom .loader import InvalidEvalId, validate_eval_id\n105\t\n106\t__all__ = [\n107\t    \"SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH\",\n108\t    \"HomeContainmentViolation\",\n109\t    \"HomeIsolation\",\n110\t    \"containment_guard\",\n111\t]\n112\t\n113\t\n114\t# NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract — see ``HomeIsolation.setup`` docstring and\n115\t# D-0033/spec.md. The tag is written under the per-eval HOME so that the\n116\t# directory becomes self-describing for downstream consumers (the orchestrator,\n117\t# the EvalRunner, post-mortem operators): a HOME that carries this relative\n118\t# path is one whose setup raised AFTER ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` materialized the\n119\t# directory and BEFORE ``setup`` returned successfully. The constant is\n120\t# exported so :class:`EvalRunner` (T03.x) and forensics tooling can locate the\n121\t# tag without re-deriving the path.\n122\tSETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH = \".eval-meta/setup_failed\"\n123\t\n124\t\n125\tdef _write_setup_failed_tag(home_path: Path, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n126\t    \"\"\"Write the NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` artifact tag under ``home_path``.\n127\t\n128\t    The tag distinguishes \"harness bug or environmental refusal during\n129\t    setup\" (status ERRORED) from \"eval ran and produced a failing\n130\t    assertion\" (status FAIL). The contents are split for grep-ability:\n131\t\n132\t    * Line 1 is the exception class name verbatim\n133\t      (``type(exc).__name__``), so a downstream EvalRunner can bucket\n134\t      the error with a single ``head -n1`` read.\n135\t    * The remainder is the formatted traceback for forensic inspection.\n136\t\n137\t    The helper is best-effort: ``setup`` catches and discards any\n138\t    exception this function raises so a write failure (e.g. the\n139\t    ``.eval-meta`` directory cannot be created because the per-eval HOME\n140\t    was made read-only by a future hardening step) never masks the\n141\t    original setup exception.\n142\t\n143\t    Args:\n144\t        home_path: The per-eval HOME ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n145\t            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. The tag is materialized at\n146\t            ``home_path / SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH``.\n147\t        exc: The exception that interrupted ``setup``. Used only to\n148\t            populate the tag contents; this helper never re-raises it.\n149\t    \"\"\"\n150\t\n151\t    tag_path = home_path / SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH\n152\t    tag_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n153\t    # ``traceback.format_exception`` reads the active exception state when\n154\t    # the helper is invoked from inside an ``except`` clause. Pin the\n155\t    # arguments explicitly so the helper also works when called outside\n156\t    # one (defensive — the only call site is currently inside ``except``).\n157\t    tb_lines = traceback.format_exception(type(exc), exc, exc.__traceback__)\n158\t    payload = f\"{type(exc).__name__}\\n\" + \"\".join(tb_lines)\n159\t    tag_path.write_text(payload, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n160\t\n161\t\n162\tclass HomeContainmentViolation(Exception):\n163\t    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`containment_guard` when any FR-ISO2 check fails.\n164\t\n165\t    The exception is the single hard-failure surface of FR-ISO2 — it is\n166\t    the only thing :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` may raise once\n167\t    :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` has succeeded and BEFORE the hook adapter\n168\t    (T02.14) writes a single byte under the per-eval HOME. Catching this\n169\t    type at the orchestrator level is sufficient to cover all three\n170\t    containment checks (eval_id regex, scratch-root allowlist, post-\n171\t    mkdtemp symlink resolution); callers MUST NOT branch on the\n172\t    underlying :class:`InvalidEvalId` / :class:`ScratchRootViolation`\n173\t    causes — they are exposed via ``__cause__`` for forensics only.\n174\t\n175\t    The exception MUST raise before any hook deploy or eval-state\n176\t    write so NFR-SEC2 (\"hard refusal before side effects\") is upheld;\n177\t    the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is responsible for\n178\t    preserving the partial per-eval HOME with a ``setup_failed`` tag\n179\t    once it lands.\n180\t\n181\t    Attributes:\n182\t        check: Short identifier for the failed check (``\"eval_id\"``,\n183\t            ``\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``, ``\"home_path_resolution\"`` or\n184\t            ``\"home_path_escape\"``). Lets reporters bucket failures\n185\t            without parsing the human-readable message.\n186\t        home_path: The per-eval HOME path the guard was asked to\n187\t            validate. Stored verbatim as supplied by the caller (no\n188\t            resolution) so forensics keep the original input.\n189\t        scratch_root: The declared scratch root the guard checked\n190\t            ``home_path`` against. Stored verbatim.\n191\t        eval_id: The eval id under inspection. Stored verbatim\n192\t            (``repr``-rendered) so non-string smuggled scalars are\n193\t            visible in reporter output.\n194\t        detail: Free-form one-line message describing the specific\n195\t            failure. Inherits from the underlying cause when one\n196\t            exists (e.g. the ``ScratchRootViolation`` rendering).\n197\t    \"\"\"\n198\t\n199\t    def __init__(\n200\t        self,\n201\t        *,\n202\t        check: str,\n203\t        home_path: Path,\n204\t        scratch_root: Path,\n205\t        eval_id: object,\n206\t        detail: str,\n207\t    ) -> None:\n208\t        self.check = check\n209\t        self.home_path = home_path\n210\t        self.scratch_root = scratch_root\n211\t        self.eval_id = eval_id\n212\t        self.detail = detail\n213\t        super().__init__(\n214\t            f\"FR-ISO2 containment check {check!r} failed for \"\n215\t            f\"eval_id={eval_id!r} home_path={home_path!s} \"\n216\t            f\"scratch_root={scratch_root!s}: {detail}\"\n217\t        )\n218\t\n219\t\n220\tdef containment_guard(\n221\t    home_path: Path,\n222\t    scratch_root: Path,\n223\t    eval_id: str,\n224\t    *,\n225\t    config: EvalConfig,\n226\t) -> None:\n227\t    \"\"\"FR-ISO2 three-check defense-in-depth guard.\n228\t\n229\t    The guard layers three independent checks on top of the per-eval\n230\t    HOME materialization that :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` just performed.\n231\t    Each check has a distinct attack vector in scope:\n232\t\n233\t    1. **eval_id regex** — re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n234\t       The loader (T01.07) already validates the id pre-expand and\n235\t       post-expand, but a caller bypassing :class:`SuiteLoader` (e.g.,\n236\t       a programmatic test, a future REPL entry point) could construct\n237\t       a :class:`HomeIsolation` directly. The re-check closes that\n238\t       loader-bypass surface called out in NFR-SEC2.\n239\t    2. **Scratch-root allowlist** — re-applies\n240\t       :func:`resolve_scratch_root` (T01.19) against ``scratch_root``.\n241\t       The helper resolves the path with ``Path.resolve(strict=False)``\n242\t       which collapses symlinks; if ``scratch_root`` is a symlink to a\n243\t       non-allowlisted target (e.g., ``/home/user/.claude``) the\n244\t       resolved form will not match any entry in the resolved\n245\t       :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. The allowlist is\n246\t       sourced exclusively from the caller-supplied\n247\t       :class:`EvalConfig`; nothing inside this module synthesizes a\n248\t       fallback that could inject the caller's ``scratch_root``\n249\t       into the allowlist after the fact (NFR-SEC2 loader-bypass\n250\t       defense — see D-0029 spec rationale).\n251\t    3. **Post-mkdtemp path containment** — resolves ``home_path`` with\n252\t       ``Path.resolve(strict=True)`` and asserts the resolved form is\n253\t       equal to OR a sub-path of the resolved ``scratch_root``. The\n254\t       ``strict=True`` mode raises :class:`FileNotFoundError` if the\n255\t       per-eval HOME has not been materialized yet, so this is the\n256\t       check that pins \"guard runs AFTER ``mkdtemp``\". Catches the\n257\t       attack vector where a symlinked component inside ``home_path``\n258\t       points outside ``scratch_root`` even though the textual prefix\n259\t       check would have passed.\n260\t\n261\t    On any failure the function raises :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`\n262\t    with the failing check identifier. Underlying causes\n263\t    (:class:`InvalidEvalId`, :class:`ScratchRootViolation`,\n264\t    :class:`FileNotFoundError`) are chained via ``raise ... from`` so\n265\t    forensic tooling can still walk ``__cause__``.\n266\t\n267\t    Args:\n268\t        home_path: Path to the per-eval HOME directory ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n269\t            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Must exist on disk; the\n270\t            ``strict=True`` resolve enforces this so a caller cannot\n271\t            invoke the guard \"ahead\" of ``mkdtemp``.\n272\t        scratch_root: Scratch root under which ``home_path`` must\n273\t            reside. Need not exist (``strict=False`` resolution); for\n274\t            test ergonomics callers can pass a path that\n275\t            :class:`HomeIsolation` will create via\n276\t            ``mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`` shortly after.\n277\t        eval_id: The eval identifier the per-eval HOME corresponds to.\n278\t            Re-validated through :func:`validate_eval_id`.\n279\t        config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n280\t            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` is the sole source of truth for\n281\t            the scratch-root allowlist check. The guard refuses to\n282\t            synthesize a fallback config: a missing argument is a\n283\t            programming error, never a \"use the defaults\" cue. This\n284\t            closes the bypass where a caller could otherwise construct\n285\t            ``HomeIsolation(home_root=<attacker-controlled>)`` and have\n286\t            the guard quietly add that root to the allowlist.\n287\t\n288\t    Raises:\n289\t        HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three checks failed. The\n290\t            ``check`` attribute identifies which one; ``detail`` carries\n291\t            the human-readable cause; ``__cause__`` holds the underlying\n292\t            exception when applicable.\n293\t    \"\"\"\n294\t\n295\t    # Check 1: eval_id regex. Loader-bypass defense.\n296\t    try:\n297\t        validate_eval_id(eval_id)\n298\t    except InvalidEvalId as exc:\n299\t        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n300\t            check=\"eval_id\",\n301\t            home_path=home_path,\n302\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n303\t            eval_id=eval_id,\n304\t            detail=str(exc),\n305\t        ) from exc\n306\t\n307\t    # Check 2: scratch-root allowlist (symlink-aware via resolve_scratch_root).\n308\t    # ``config`` is required: see docstring rationale (NFR-SEC2 bypass close).\n309\t    try:\n310\t        resolved_scratch = resolve_scratch_root(scratch_root, config=config)\n311\t    except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n312\t        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n313\t            check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\",\n314\t            home_path=home_path,\n315\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n316\t            eval_id=eval_id,\n317\t            detail=str(exc),\n318\t        ) from exc\n319\t\n320\t    # Check 3: post-mkdtemp symlink resolution + containment under\n321\t    # scratch_root. strict=True asserts the HOME exists (i.e., mkdtemp\n322\t    # already ran). Any symlink inside home_path is collapsed; the\n323\t    # resolved form must equal or live beneath the resolved scratch root.\n324\t    try:\n325\t        resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=True)\n326\t    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n327\t        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n328\t            check=\"home_path_resolution\",\n329\t            home_path=home_path,\n330\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n331\t            eval_id=eval_id,\n332\t            detail=(\n333\t                \"home_path does not exist on disk; FR-ISO2 requires the \"\n334\t                \"guard to run AFTER mkdtemp materialized the per-eval \"\n335\t                \"HOME\"\n336\t            ),\n337\t        ) from exc\n338\t\n339\t    if not (\n340\t        resolved_home == resolved_scratch\n341\t        or resolved_home.is_relative_to(resolved_scratch)\n342\t    ):\n343\t        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n344\t            check=\"home_path_escape\",\n345\t            home_path=home_path,\n346\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n347\t            eval_id=eval_id,\n348\t            detail=(\n349\t                f\"resolved home_path {resolved_home!s} escapes resolved \"\n350\t                f\"scratch_root {resolved_scratch!s}\"\n351\t            ),\n352\t        )\n353\t\n354\t\n355\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n356\tclass HomeIsolation:\n357\t    \"\"\"Per-eval HOME isolation record + COMP-006 method surface.\n358\t\n359\t    DM-006 fixes the immutable four-field record contract:\n360\t\n361\t    * ``eval_id``        — ``str``, required. Re-validated in\n362\t      ``__post_init__`` via :func:`validate_eval_id` so any unsafe id\n363\t      raises :class:`superclaude.cli.eval.InvalidEvalId` BEFORE any\n364\t      consumer can derive a filesystem path from it.\n365\t    * ``home_root``      — ``pathlib.Path``, required. Scratch root under\n366\t      which the per-eval HOME is created by :meth:`setup`. The dataclass\n367\t      stores it as-is; full containment is FR-ISO2's responsibility\n368\t      (T02.08).\n369\t    * ``session_id``     — ``str``, required. Value :meth:`env` stamps\n370\t      into ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` for the subprocess. Allocation lives in\n371\t      the orchestrator (FR-G2 / T03.16); this record only holds the\n372\t      assigned value.\n373\t    * ``time_offset_sec`` — ``int``, defaults to ``0``. Optional simulated\n374\t      wall-clock offset in whole seconds; :meth:`env` emits\n375\t      ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when non-zero. Full activation is\n376\t      gated on OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8 / T06.03).\n377\t\n378\t    The four declared fields are frozen — mutation raises\n379\t    ``dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError``. A single private slot,\n380\t    ``_home_path``, tracks the post-:meth:`setup` HOME directory; it is\n381\t    written via ``object.__setattr__`` (the documented escape hatch for\n382\t    frozen dataclasses) and never participates in equality or hashing.\n383\t    \"\"\"\n384\t\n385\t    eval_id: str\n386\t    home_root: Path\n387\t    session_id: str\n388\t    time_offset_sec: int = 0\n389\t\n390\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n391\t        \"\"\"Re-apply the FR-SCH2 guard and initialize the private path slot.\n392\t\n393\t        The FR-SCH2 re-validation makes loader-bypass impossible:\n394\t        constructing a ``HomeIsolation`` with an unsafe ``eval_id``\n395\t        raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` before any filesystem operation\n396\t        becomes reachable. The private ``_home_path`` slot is set to\n397\t        ``None`` so :meth:`env` / :meth:`state_path` can raise a clear\n398\t        error when called before :meth:`setup`.\n399\t        \"\"\"\n400\t\n401\t        validate_eval_id(self.eval_id)\n402\t        object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", None)\n403\t\n404\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n405\t    # COMP-006 method surface (FR-ISO1)\n406\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n407\t\n408\t    @property\n409\t    def home_path(self) -> Path:\n410\t        \"\"\"Return the per-eval HOME directory created by :meth:`setup`.\n411\t\n412\t        Raises :class:`RuntimeError` when :meth:`setup` has not yet been\n413\t        called or when :meth:`teardown` has already cleared the slot,\n414\t        so the failure mode is \"obvious AttributeError\" instead of a\n415\t        silent ``None`` flowing into an env dict.\n416\t        \"\"\"\n417\t\n418\t        home = self._home_path  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n419\t        if home is None:\n420\t            raise RuntimeError(\n421\t                \"HomeIsolation.setup() must be called before accessing home_path\"\n422\t            )\n423\t        return home\n424\t\n425\t    @property\n426\t    def is_set_up(self) -> bool:\n427\t        \"\"\"Return ``True`` when :meth:`setup` has created a per-eval HOME\n428\t        and :meth:`teardown` has not yet been called.\n429\t\n430\t        Lets callers (atomic setup wrapper T02.13, the orchestrator)\n431\t        ask whether teardown is needed without catching exceptions.\n432\t        \"\"\"\n433\t\n434\t        return self._home_path is not None  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n435\t\n436\t    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n437\t        \"\"\"Create the per-eval HOME directory under ``home_root``.\n438\t\n439\t        Uses :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` with the eval_id as prefix so\n440\t        sibling per-eval HOMEs under the same ``home_root`` are\n441\t        guaranteed to be unique by the OS (atomic on POSIX, see\n442\t        ``mkdtemp`` docs). Parallel evals never collide on directory\n443\t        names; mutating one HOME cannot affect siblings because each\n444\t        HOME is its own ``mkdtemp`` root.\n445\t\n446\t        After ``mkdtemp`` succeeds, :func:`containment_guard` (FR-ISO2,\n447\t        T02.08) is applied AFTER the per-eval HOME has been\n448\t        materialized and BEFORE any hook deploy or eval-state write.\n449\t        The guard re-validates ``eval_id`` against the FR-SCH2 regex,\n450\t        re-applies the AC12 scratch-root allowlist, and\n451\t        ``Path.resolve(strict=True)``-checks the freshly created HOME\n452\t        against the scratch root. Any failure surfaces as\n453\t        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`; the partial per-eval HOME is\n454\t        left on disk so callers can route teardown through\n455\t        ``teardown(keep=True)`` for forensic inspection (the\n456\t        containment exception itself carries the full forensic\n457\t        payload — see NFR-ISO2 contract below).\n458\t\n459\t        Idempotency rule: calling :meth:`setup` twice on the same\n460\t        instance raises :class:`RuntimeError`. This prevents the silent\n461\t        directory leak that would otherwise occur if a caller looped\n462\t        over an instance, and it makes the atomic-setup wrapper\n463\t        (T02.13) easier to reason about.\n464\t\n465\t        Args:\n466\t            config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n467\t                ``allowed_scratch_roots`` provides the scratch-root\n468\t                allowlist :func:`containment_guard` enforces.\n469\t                ``setup`` deliberately refuses to synthesize a\n470\t                fallback that injects ``self.home_root`` into the\n471\t                allowlist — that would defeat the AC12 check, because a\n472\t                caller can construct ``HomeIsolation`` with any\n473\t                ``home_root`` (per-instance, the dataclass cannot\n474\t                meaningfully validate it). Production callers\n475\t                (orchestrator T03.16) pass the run's resolved config;\n476\t                tests build an :class:`EvalConfig` whose allowlist\n477\t                includes the test scratch root.\n478\t\n479\t        Returns:\n480\t            The freshly created per-eval HOME path (also recorded on\n481\t            :attr:`home_path`).\n482\t\n483\t        Raises:\n484\t            RuntimeError: If :meth:`setup` was already called on this\n485\t                instance and :meth:`teardown` has not run since.\n486\t            HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three FR-ISO2 checks\n487\t                failed; the per-eval HOME directory was created and is\n488\t                preserved on disk for inspection with a\n489\t                ``setup_failed`` tag (NFR-ISO2 — see below).\n490\t\n491\t        NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract (T02.13 / D-0033)\n492\t        ------------------------------------------------\n493\t\n494\t        Any exception raised AFTER :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` and BEFORE\n495\t        ``setup`` returns successfully MUST leave the per-eval HOME on\n496\t        disk. The private ``_home_path`` slot stays populated so a\n497\t        caller that wraps ``setup`` in ``try/finally`` can route\n498\t        teardown through ``teardown(keep=True)`` to preserve the\n499\t        partial HOME for forensics.\n500\t\n501\t        Non-containment exceptions additionally drop a ``setup_failed``\n502\t        artifact tag at ``<home>/.eval-meta/setup_failed`` (constant\n503\t        :data:`SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH`). The tag's first line is the\n504\t        exception class name; the body is the formatted traceback. The\n505\t        tag distinguishes harness bugs / environmental refusal (status\n506\t        ERRORED — tag present, no eval output) from real eval failures\n507\t        (status FAIL — eval ran, expectations missed). The tag write\n508\t        is best-effort: a secondary exception during tag write is\n509\t        discarded so the original exception is the one the caller\n510\t        sees. The original exception type is preserved verbatim — the\n511\t        wrapper adds the tag side effect, it does not re-wrap or\n512\t        re-classify the exception.\n513\t\n514\t        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` is the deliberate exception\n515\t        to the tag-write rule: writing under a HOME the guard just\n516\t        refused could land under the real ``~/.claude/`` directory\n517\t        when the scratch root or the ``mkdtemp`` result is symlinked\n518\t        there (the attack matrix exercised by NFR-SEC3 in T02.10).\n519\t        The violation exception itself is the forensic signal — its\n520\t        ``check``, ``home_path``, ``scratch_root``, ``eval_id`` and\n521\t        ``detail`` attributes carry everything a reporter needs to\n522\t        bucket the eval as ERRORED.\n523\t        \"\"\"\n524\t\n525\t        if self._home_path is not None:  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n526\t            raise RuntimeError(\n527\t                f\"HomeIsolation.setup() already called for eval_id={self.eval_id!r}\"\n528\t            )\n529\t\n530\t        # Ensure the scratch root exists. ``parents=True, exist_ok=True``\n531\t        # is safe because the FR-ISO2 guard below catches any path that\n532\t        # resolves outside the policy allowlist.\n533\t        self.home_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n534\t\n535\t        home = Path(\n536\t            tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{self.eval_id}-\", dir=str(self.home_root))\n537\t        )\n538\t        object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", home)\n539\t\n540\t        # NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper. The ``_home_path`` slot stays populated for the\n546\t        # same reason.\n547\t        #\n548\t        # Tag-write asymmetry between containment and non-containment\n549\t        # exceptions:\n550\t        #\n551\t        # * :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` — NO tag is written.\n552\t        #   Writing a file under a HOME the guard just refused would\n553\t        #   breach the NFR-SEC3 invariants enforced in T02.10\n554\t        #   (`test_hard_guard_real_home.py`): when ``scratch_root`` is\n555\t        #   (or symlinks to) the real ``~/.claude/``, or when\n556\t        #   :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` returned a symlinked path that\n557\t        #   resolves outside ``scratch_root``, any write under ``home``\n558\t        #   lands inside real ``~/.claude/``. The containment exception\n559\t        #   itself carries the full forensic payload (``check``,\n560\t        #   ``home_path``, ``scratch_root``, ``eval_id``, ``detail``)\n561\t        #   so the orchestrator can bucket the eval as ERRORED without\n562\t        #   the tag.\n563\t        # * Any other ``Exception`` — tag is written. These are\n564\t        #   harness bugs or post-containment failures (e.g., hook deploy\n565\t        #   verification under T02.14) where the per-eval HOME is known\n566\t        #   to be inside the allowlisted scratch root and the tag is\n567\t        #   safe to write. The tag write is best-effort: a secondary\n568\t        #   failure is swallowed so the original exception surfaces\n569\t        #   unchanged.\n570\t        try:\n571\t            # FR-ISO2 defense-in-depth: re-check eval_id, scratch-root\n572\t            # allowlist, and symlink-resolved containment AFTER mkdtemp\n573\t            # and BEFORE the hook adapter (T02.14) writes anything under\n574\t            # the per-eval HOME.\n575\t            containment_guard(\n576\t                home_path=home,\n577\t                scratch_root=self.home_root,\n578\t                eval_id=self.eval_id,\n579\t                config=config,\n580\t            )\n581\t        except HomeContainmentViolation:\n582\t            # NFR-SEC3: no FS writes under a refused HOME. The\n583\t            # containment exception itself is the structured signal.\n584\t            raise\n585\t        except Exception as exc:\n586\t            try:\n587\t                _write_setup_failed_tag(home, exc)\n588\t            except Exception:\n589\t                pass\n590\t            raise\n591\t        return home\n592\t\n593\t    def env(self) -> dict[str, str]:\n594\t        \"\"\"Return the env-var dict the subprocess needs for HOME isolation.\n595\t\n596\t        * ``HOME`` is always present.\n597\t        * ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` is always present (FR-ISO1 mandate).\n598\t        * ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` is present only when\n599\t          ``time_offset_sec`` differs from the default ``0``. The COMP-012 probe at\n``tests/cli/eval/test_isolation_layers_probe.py`` (Task T02.05) must keep\npassing after this extension, which it does because nothing in\n``cli/sprint/executor.py`` is touched.\n\nDM-006 record (T02.04) — the four immutable fields\n====================================================\n\n``HomeIsolation`` is a ``@dataclass(frozen=True)`` so once an instance is\nbuilt it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers. This makes it safe to\nshare across threads inside the parallel orchestrator (R-058 / T03.16)\nwithout locking. Equality is structural across all four fields via the\ndataclass-generated ``__eq__``.\n\n1. ``eval_id``         — the FR-SCH2-validated eval identifier. The id\n   flows directly into the per-eval HOME by :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`\n   so it MUST satisfy the FR-SCH2 regex before any filesystem write. The\n   ``__post_init__`` hook re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05) so\n   a ``HomeIsolation`` can never be constructed with an unsafe id even\n   when a caller bypasses the loader.\n2. ``home_root``       — the scratch root directory under which the\n   per-eval HOME is ``mkdtemp``-ed by :meth:`setup`. Full FR-ISO2 path\n   containment lands in T02.08; this module performs only the minimal\n   ``parents=True, exist_ok=True`` materialization needed to create\n   sibling per-eval HOMEs concurrently.\n3. ``session_id``      — the value :meth:`env` stamps into\n   ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID``. Allocation lives in the orchestrator (FR-G2 /\n   T03.16); this record only holds the assigned value.\n4. ``time_offset_sec`` — optional offset in whole seconds for the eval's\n   simulated wall clock. Default ``0`` matches DM-006 verbatim; when the\n   value is non-zero :meth:`env` adds ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET``. Whether\n   the subprocess actually honors the variable is gated on OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8\n   / T06.03); keeping the offset opt-in via non-zero values means the\n   env dict stays minimal until OQ-8 resolves.\n\nCOMP-006 method surface (T02.07) — FR-ISO1\n==========================================\n\nThe four methods below are the externally observable surface of\nCOMP-006. They mutate process-local filesystem state (and an internal\nprivate slot tracking the created HOME path) without ever mutating the\nfour DM-006 fields:\n\n* :meth:`setup`                 — ``mkdtemp`` under ``home_root`` and stash\n  the created path on a private ``_home_path`` slot via\n  ``object.__setattr__``. Returns the created path. Idempotency is one-\n  shot per instance: calling :meth:`setup` twice raises ``RuntimeError``\n  rather than silently leaking a directory.\n* :meth:`env`                   — returns the env-var dict the subprocess\n  needs: ``HOME`` always, ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` always, and\n  ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when ``time_offset_sec != 0``. The\n  return type pins ``dict[str, str]`` so callers can ``.update()`` it\n  alongside ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` without coercion.\n* :meth:`teardown`              — removes the per-eval HOME when ``keep``\n  is false, leaves it on disk when ``keep`` is true (so post-mortem\n  inspection or NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` tagging in T02.13 can still\n  pick the directory up). Always clears the private path slot so the\n  instance is safe to garbage-collect.\n* :meth:`state_path`            — joins ``suffix`` under the active\n  per-eval HOME so consumers (hook adapter T02.14, atomic setup T02.13,\n  artifact writers) never need to import :mod:`os.path` themselves. The\n  full containment check on ``suffix`` lands in T02.08; this method\n  performs the minimum necessary check that the joined path stays under\n  ``home_path``.\n\nCross-links\n===========\n\n* DM-006 record spec (roadmap row 26).\n* COMP-006 method surface (roadmap rows 28 / 32; T02.07 / T02.11).\n* FR-SCH2 :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n* FR-ISO2 ``containment_guard`` (T02.08) — strengthens the symlink /\n  allowlist / regex checks layered on top of :meth:`setup` and\n  :meth:`state_path`.\n* DOC-OQ8 (T06.03) — gates ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` semantics.\n* COMP-012 probe (T02.05 — ``test_isolation_layers_probe.py``) — pins the\n  upstream ``IsolationLayers`` shape this module sits beside.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nimport tempfile\nimport traceback\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom .config import EvalConfig, ScratchRootViolation, resolve_scratch_root\nfrom .loader import InvalidEvalId, validate_eval_id\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH\",\n    \"HomeContainmentViolation\",\n    \"HomeIsolation\",\n    \"containment_guard\",\n]\n\n\n# NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract — see ``HomeIsolation.setup`` docstring and\n# D-0033/spec.md. The tag is written under the per-eval HOME so that the\n# directory becomes self-describing for downstream consumers (the orchestrator,\n# the EvalRunner, post-mortem operators): a HOME that carries this relative\n# path is one whose setup raised AFTER ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` materialized the\n# directory and BEFORE ``setup`` returned successfully. The constant is\n# exported so :class:`EvalRunner` (T03.x) and forensics tooling can locate the\n# tag without re-deriving the path.\nSETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH = \".eval-meta/setup_failed\"\n\n\ndef _write_setup_failed_tag(home_path: Path, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Write the NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` artifact tag under ``home_path``.\n\n    The tag distinguishes \"harness bug or environmental refusal during\n    setup\" (status ERRORED) from \"eval ran and produced a failing\n    assertion\" (status FAIL). The contents are split for grep-ability:\n\n    * Line 1 is the exception class name verbatim\n      (``type(exc).__name__``), so a downstream EvalRunner can bucket\n      the error with a single ``head -n1`` read.\n    * The remainder is the formatted traceback for forensic inspection.\n\n    The helper is best-effort: ``setup`` catches and discards any\n    exception this function raises so a write failure (e.g. the\n    ``.eval-meta`` directory cannot be created because the per-eval HOME\n    was made read-only by a future hardening step) never masks the\n    original setup exception.\n\n    Args:\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. The tag is materialized at\n            ``home_path / SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH``.\n        exc: The exception that interrupted ``setup``. Used only to\n            populate the tag contents; this helper never re-raises it.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    tag_path = home_path / SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH\n    tag_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    # ``traceback.format_exception`` reads the active exception state when\n    # the helper is invoked from inside an ``except`` clause. Pin the\n    # arguments explicitly so the helper also works when called outside\n    # one (defensive — the only call site is currently inside ``except``).\n    tb_lines = traceback.format_exception(type(exc), exc, exc.__traceback__)\n    payload = f\"{type(exc).__name__}\\n\" + \"\".join(tb_lines)\n    tag_path.write_text(payload, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n\nclass HomeContainmentViolation(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`containment_guard` when any FR-ISO2 check fails.\n\n    The exception is the single hard-failure surface of FR-ISO2 — it is\n    the only thing :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` may raise once\n    :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` has succeeded and BEFORE the hook adapter\n    (T02.14) writes a single byte under the per-eval HOME. Catching this\n    type at the orchestrator level is sufficient to cover all three\n    containment checks (eval_id regex, scratch-root allowlist, post-\n    mkdtemp symlink resolution); callers MUST NOT branch on the\n    underlying :class:`InvalidEvalId` / :class:`ScratchRootViolation`\n    causes — they are exposed via ``__cause__`` for forensics only.\n\n    The exception MUST raise before any hook deploy or eval-state\n    write so NFR-SEC2 (\"hard refusal before side effects\") is upheld;\n    the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is responsible for\n    preserving the partial per-eval HOME with a ``setup_failed`` tag\n    once it lands.\n\n    Attributes:\n        check: Short identifier for the failed check (``\"eval_id\"``,\n            ``\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``, ``\"home_path_resolution\"`` or\n            ``\"home_path_escape\"``). Lets reporters bucket failures\n            without parsing the human-readable message.\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME path the guard was asked to\n            validate. Stored verbatim\n            (``repr``-rendered) so non-string smuggled scalars are\n            visible in reporter output.\n        detail: Free-form one-line message describing the specific\n            failure. Inherits from the underlying cause when one\n            exists (e.g. the ``ScratchRootViolation`` rendering).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        check: str,\n        home_path: Path,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        eval_id: object,\n        detail: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        self.check = check\n        self.home_path = home_path\n        self.scratch_root = scratch_root\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        self.detail = detail\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"FR-ISO2 containment check {check!r} failed for \"\n            f\"eval_id={eval_id!r} home_path={home_path!s} \"\n            f\"scratch_root={scratch_root!s}: {detail}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef containment_guard(\n    home_path: Path,\n    scratch_root: Path,\n    eval_id: str,\n    *,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-ISO2 three-check defense-in-depth guard.\n\n    The guard layers three independent checks on top of the per-eval\n    HOME materialization that :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` just performed.\n    Each check has a distinct attack vector in scope:\n\n    1. **eval_id regex** — re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n       The loader (T01.07) already validates the id pre-expand and\n       post-expand, but a caller bypassing :class:`SuiteLoader` (e.g.,\n       a programmatic test, a future REPL entry point) could construct\n       a :class:`HomeIsolation` directly. The re-check closes that\n       loader-bypass surface called out in NFR-SEC2.\n    2. **Scratch-root allowlist** — re-applies\n       :func:`resolve_scratch_root` (T01.19) against ``scratch_root``.\n       The helper resolves the path with ``Path.resolve(strict=False)``\n       which collapses symlinks; if ``scratch_root`` is a symlink to a\n       non-allowlisted target (e.g., ``/home/user/.claude``) the\n       resolved form will not match any entry in the resolved\n       :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. The allowlist is\n       sourced exclusively from the caller-supplied\n       :class:`EvalConfig`; nothing inside this module synthesizes a\n       fallback that could inject the caller's ``scratch_root``\n       into the allowlist after the fact (NFR-SEC2 loader-bypass\n       defense — see D-0029 spec rationale).\n    3. **Post-mkdtemp path containment** — resolves ``home_path`` with\n       ``Path.resolve(strict=True)`` and asserts the resolved form is\n       equal to OR a sub-path of the resolved ``scratch_root``. The\n       ``strict=True`` mode raises :class:`FileNotFoundError` if the\n       per-eval HOME has not been materialized yet, so this is the\n       check that pins \"guard runs AFTER ``mkdtemp``\". Catches the\n       attack vector where a symlinked component inside ``home_path``\n       points outside ``scratch_root`` even though the textual prefix\n       check would have passed.\n\n    On any failure the function raises :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`\n    with the failing check identifier. Underlying causes\n    (:class:`InvalidEvalId`, :class:`ScratchRootViolation`,\n    :class:`FileNotFoundError`) are chained via ``raise ... from`` so\n    forensic tooling can still walk ``__cause__``.\n\n    Args:\n        home_path: Path to the per-eval HOME directory ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Must exist on disk; the\n            ``strict=True`` resolve enforces this so a caller cannot\n            invoke the guard \"ahead\" of ``mkdtemp``.\n        scratch_root: Scratch root under which ``home_path`` must\n            reside. Need not exist (``strict=False`` resolution); for\n            test ergonomics callers can pass a path that\n            :class:`HomeIsolation` will create via\n            ``mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`` shortly after.\n        eval_id: The eval identifier the per-eval HOME corresponds to.\n            Re-validated through :func:`validate_eval_id`.\n        config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` is the sole source of truth for\n            the scratch-root allowlist check. The guard refuses to\n            synthesize a fallback config: a missing argument is a\n            programming error, never a \"use the defaults\" cue. This\n            closes the bypass where a caller could otherwise construct\n            ``HomeIsolation(home_root=<attacker-controlled>)`` and have\n            the guard quietly add that root to the allowlist.\n\n    Raises:\n        HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three checks failed. The\n            ``check`` attribute identifies which one; ``detail`` carries\n            the human-readable cause; ``__cause__`` holds the underlying\n            exception when applicable.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Check 1: eval_id regex. Loader-bypass defense.\n    try:\n        validate_eval_id(eval_id)\n    except InvalidEvalId as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"eval_id\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=str(exc),\n        ) from exc\n\n    # Check 2: scratch-root allowlist (symlink-aware via resolve_scratch_root).\n    # ``config`` is required: see docstring rationale (NFR-SEC2 bypass close).\n    try:\n        resolved_scratch = resolve_scratch_root(scratch_root, config=config)\n    except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=str(exc),\n        ) from exc\n\n    # Check 3: post-mkdtemp symlink resolution + containment under\n    # scratch_root. strict=True asserts the HOME exists (i.e., mkdtemp\n    # already ran). Any symlink inside home_path is collapsed; the\n    # resolved form must equal or live beneath the resolved scratch root.\n    try:\n        resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=True)\n    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"home_path_resolution\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=(\n                \"home_path does not exist on disk; FR-ISO2 requires the \"\n                \"guard to run AFTER mkdtemp materialized the per-eval \"\n                \"HOME\"\n            ),\n        ) from exc\n\n    if not (\n        resolved_home == resolved_scratch\n        or resolved_home.is_relative_to(resolved_scratch)\n    ):\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"home_path_escape\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=(\n                f\"resolved home_path {resolved_home!s} escapes resolved \"\n                f\"scratch_root {resolved_scratch!s}\"\n            ),\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass HomeIsolation:\n    \"\"\"Per-eval HOME isolation record + COMP-006 method surface.\n\n    DM-006 fixes the immutable four-field record contract:\n\n    * ``eval_id``        — ``str``, required. Re-validated in\n      ``__post_init__`` via :func:`validate_eval_id` so any unsafe id\n      raises :class:`superclaude.cli.eval.InvalidEvalId` BEFORE any\n      consumer can derive a filesystem path from it.\n    * ``home_root``      — ``pathlib.Path``, required. Scratch root under\n      which the per-eval HOME is created by :meth:`setup`. The dataclass\n      stores it as-is; full containment is FR-ISO2's responsibility\n      (T02.08).\n    * ``session_id``     — ``str``, required. Value :meth:`env` stamps\n      into ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` for the subprocess. Allocation lives in\n      the orchestrator (FR-G2 / T03.16); this record only holds the\n      assigned value.\n    * ``time_offset_sec`` — ``int``, defaults to ``0``. Optional simulated\n      wall-clock offset in whole seconds; :meth:`env` emits\n      ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when non-zero. Full activation is\n      gated on OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8 / T06.03).\n\n    The four declared fields are frozen — mutation raises\n    ``dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError``. A single private slot,\n    ``_home_path``, tracks the post-:meth:`setup` HOME directory; it is\n    written via ``object.__setattr__`` (the documented escape hatch for\n    frozen dataclasses) and never participates in equality or hashing.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    home_root: Path\n    session_id: str\n    time_offset_sec: int = 0\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Re-apply the FR-SCH2 guard and initialize the private path slot.\n\n        The FR-SCH2 re-validation makes loader-bypass impossible:\n        constructing a ``HomeIsolation`` with an unsafe ``eval_id``\n        raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` before any filesystem operation\n        becomes reachable. The private ``_home_path`` slot is set to\n        ``None`` so :meth:`env` / :meth:`state_path` can raise a clear\n        error when called before :meth:`setup`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        validate_eval_id(self.eval_id)\n        object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", None)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # COMP-006 method surface (FR-ISO1)\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def home_path(self) -> Path:\n        \"\"\"Return the per-eval HOME directory created by :meth:`setup`.\n\n        Raises :class:`RuntimeError` when :meth:`setup` has not yet been\n        called or when :meth:`teardown` has already cleared the slot,\n        so the failure mode is \"obvious AttributeError\" instead of a\n        silent ``None`` flowing into an env dict.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        home = self._home_path  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n        if home is None:\n            raise RuntimeError(\n                \"HomeIsolation.setup() must be called before accessing home_path\"\n            )\n        return home\n\n    @property\n    def is_set_up(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` when :meth:`setup` has created a per-eval HOME\n        and :meth:`teardown` has not yet been called.\n\n        Lets callers (atomic setup wrapper T02.13, the orchestrator)\n        ask whether teardown is needed without catching exceptions.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return self._home_path is not None  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n\n    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n        \"\"\"Create the per-eval HOME directory under ``home_root``.\n\n        Uses :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` with the eval_id as prefix so\n        sibling per-eval HOMEs under the same ``home_root`` are\n        guaranteed to be unique by the OS (atomic on POSIX, see\n        ``mkdtemp`` docs). Parallel evals never collide on directory\n        names; mutating one HOME cannot affect siblings because each\n        HOME is its own ``mkdtemp`` root.\n\n        After ``mkdtemp`` succeeds, :func:`containment_guard` (FR-ISO2,\n        T02.08) is applied AFTER the per-eval HOME has been\n        materialized and BEFORE any hook deploy or eval-state write.\n        The guard re-validates ``eval_id`` against the FR-SCH2 regex,\n        re-applies the AC12 scratch-root allowlist, and\n        ``Path.resolve(strict=True)``-checks the freshly created HOME\n        against the scratch root. Any failure surfaces as\n        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`; the partial per-eval HOME is\n        left on disk so callers can route teardown through\n        ``teardown(keep=True)`` for forensic inspection (the\n        containment exception itself carries the full forensic\n        payload — see NFR-ISO2 contract below).\n\n        Idempotency rule: calling :meth:`setup` twice on the same\n        instance raises :class:`RuntimeError`. This prevents the silent\n        directory leak that would otherwise occur if a caller looped\n        over an instance, and it makes the atomic-setup wrapper\n        (T02.13) easier to reason about.\n\n        Args:\n            config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n                ``allowed_scratch_roots`` provides the scratch-root\n                allowlist :func:`containment_guard` enforces.\n                ``setup`` deliberately refuses to synthesize a\n                fallback that injects ``self.home_root`` into the\n                allowlist — that would defeat the AC12 check, because a\n                caller can construct ``HomeIsolation`` with any\n                ``home_root`` (per-instance, the dataclass cannot\n                meaningfully validate it). Production callers\n                (orchestrator T03.16) pass the run's resolved config;\n                tests build an :class:`EvalConfig` whose allowlist\n                includes the test scratch root.\n\n        Returns:\n            The freshly created per-eval HOME path (also recorded on\n            :attr:`home_path`).\n\n        Raises:\n            RuntimeError: If :meth:`setup` was already called on this\n                instance and :meth:`teardown` has not run since.\n            HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three FR-ISO2 checks\n                failed; the per-eval HOME directory was created and is\n                preserved on disk for inspection with a\n                ``setup_failed`` tag (NFR-ISO2 — see below).\n\n        NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract (T02.13 / D-0033)\n        ------------------------------------------------\n\n        Any exception raised AFTER :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` and BEFORE\n        ``setup`` returns successfully MUST leave the per-eval HOME on\n        disk. The private ``_home_path`` slot stays populated so a\n        caller that wraps ``setup`` in ``try/finally`` can route\n        teardown through ``teardown(keep=True)`` to preserve the\n        partial HOME for forensics.\n\n        Non-containment exceptions additionally drop a ``setup_failed``\n        artifact tag at ``<home>/.eval-meta/setup_failed`` (constant\n        :data:`SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH`). The tag's first line is the\n        exception class name; the body is the formatted traceback. The\n        tag distinguishes harness bugs / environmental refusal (status\n        ERRORED — tag present, no eval output) from real eval failures\n        (status FAIL — eval ran, expectations missed). The tag write\n        is best-effort: a secondary exception during tag write is\n        discarded so the original exception is the one the caller\n        sees. The original exception type is preserved verbatim — the\n        wrapper adds the tag side effect, it does not re-wrap or\n        re-classify the exception.\n\n        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` is the deliberate exception\n        to the tag-write rule: writing under a HOME the guard just\n        refused could land under the real ``~/.claude/`` directory\n        when the scratch root or the ``mkdtemp`` result is symlinked\n        there (the attack matrix exercised by NFR-SEC3 in T02.10).\n        The violation exception itself is the forensic signal — its\n        ``check``, ``home_path``, ``scratch_root``, ``eval_id`` and\n        ``detail`` attributes carry everything a reporter needs to\n        bucket the eval as ERRORED.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._home_path is not None:  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n            raise RuntimeError(\n                f\"HomeIsolation.setup() already called for eval_id={self.eval_id!r}\"\n            )\n\n        # Ensure the scratch root exists. ``parents=True, exist_ok=True``\n        # is safe because the FR-ISO2 guard below catches any path that\n        # resolves outside the policy allowlist.\n        self.home_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n        home = Path(\n            tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{self.eval_id}-\", dir=str(self.home_root))\n        )\n        object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", home)\n\n        # NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper. Anything that raises beyond this\n        # line — FR-ISO2 containment_guard today, any future post-mkdtemp\n        # work (hook deploy verification, capability stamping) tomorrow —\n        # leaves the per-eval HOME on disk so ``teardown(keep=True)``\n        # preserves the directory for post-mortem inspection without a\n        # re-resolve. The ``_home_path`` slot stays populated for the\n        # same reason.\n        #\n        # Tag-write asymmetry between containment and non-containment\n        # exceptions:\n        #\n        # * :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` — NO tag is written.\n        #   Writing a file under a HOME the guard just refused would\n        #   breach the NFR-SEC3 invariants enforced in T02.10\n        #   (`test_hard_guard_real_home.py`): when ``scratch_root`` is\n        #   (or symlinks to) the real ``~/.claude/``, or when\n        #   :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` returned a symlinked path that\n        #   resolves outside ``scratch_root``, any write under ``home``\n        #   lands inside real ``~/.claude/``. The containment exception\n        #   itself carries the full forensic payload (``check``,\n        #   ``home_path``, ``scratch_root``, ``eval_id``, ``detail``)\n        #   so the orchestrator can bucket the eval as ERRORED without\n        #   the tag.\n        # * Any other ``Exception`` — tag is written. These are\n        #   harness bugs or post-containment failures (e.g., hook deploy\n        #   verification under T02.14) where the per-eval HOME is known\n        #   to be inside the allowlisted scratch root and the tag is\n        #   safe to write. The tag write is best-effort: a secondary\n        #   failure is swallowed so the original exception surfaces\n        #   unchanged.\n        try:\n            # FR-ISO2 defense-in-depth: re-check eval_id, scratch-root\n            # allowlist, and symlink-resolved containment AFTER mkdtemp\n            # and BEFORE the hook adapter (T02.14) writes anything under\n            # the per-eval HOME.\n            containment_guard(\n                home_path=home,\n                scratch_root=self.home_root,\n                eval_id=self.eval_id,\n                config=config,\n            )\n        except HomeContainmentViolation:\n            # NFR-SEC3: no FS writes under a refused HOME. The\n            # containment exception itself is the structured signal.\n            raise\n        except Exception as exc:\n            try:\n                _write_setup_failed_tag(home, exc)\n            except Exception:\n                pass\n            raise\n        return home\n\n    def env(self) -> dict[str, str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the env-var dict the subprocess needs for HOME isolation.\n\n        * ``HOME`` is always present.\n        * ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` is always present (FR-ISO1 mandate).\n        * ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` is present only when\n          ``time_offset_sec`` differs from the default ``0``. This pins the failure surface unambiguously to\n    # the containment guard rather than to an incidental\n    # ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` ``OSError`` raised by the kernel before the\n    # guard runs. The containment guard's eval_id check must\n        surface a :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with\n        ``check=\"eval_id\"`` and :class:`InvalidEvalId` chained as\n        ``__cause__``.\"\"\"\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",  # safe at construction\n            home_root=scratch_root,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-3\",\n        )\n\n        # Bypass the dataclass freeze the same way an attacker (or an\n        # accidental ``__setattr__`` shim) would.\n        object.__setattr__(iso, \"eval_id\", tampered_eval_id)\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=permissive_config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"eval_id\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, InvalidEvalId)\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == tampered_eval_id\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 4 — loader-bypass: caller skips SuiteLoader entirely\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorLoaderBypass:\n    \"\"\"Vector 4 (``loader-bypass``).\n\n    The FR-SCH2 regex enforcement lives in\n    :func:`validate_eval_id` and is applied by\n    :class:`SuiteLoader` on every parsed-and-expanded eval id (T01.05\n    + T01.07). NFR-SEC2 mandates that a caller skipping the loader —\n    for example a programmatic test, a future REPL entry point, or an\n    accidentally instantiated ``HomeIsolation`` in an integration\n    fixture — STILL fails closed. Two layers enforce this:\n\n    1. ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` re-runs\n       :func:`validate_eval_id` at construction.\n    2. :func:`containment_guard` re-runs\n       :func:`validate_eval_id` inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`.\n\n    The first layer raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` directly because\n    ``__post_init__`` runs before any guard-wrapping; the second is\n    proven separately by the post-construction mutation vector above.\n    Acceptance criteria require this test to prove that *constructing*\n    ``HomeIsolation`` without going through :class:`SuiteLoader` still\n    fails containment — so we assert the constructor-time defense\n    here. :class:`InvalidEvalId` is the canonical loader-bypass\n    failure surface; in production code the CLI boundary maps it to\n    :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2). This pins the same\n    error class :func:`containment_guard` would have chained as\n    ``__cause__`` if the constructor's guard had been removed —\n    keeping the bypass closure consistent across both layers.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\n        \"loader_rejected_eval_id\",\n        [\n            \"../escape\",\n            \"/absolute/path\",\n            \"E1/with/sep\",\n            \"9bad\",\n            \"{{template}}\",\n            \"${shell}\",\n            \"E1\\nE2\",\n        ],\n    )\n    def test_construction_rejects_loader_rejected_eval_id(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        loader_rejected_eval_id: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Constructing :class:`HomeIsolation` directly with a\n        loader-rejected eval id must raise :class:`InvalidEvalId`\n        before any filesystem operation becomes reachable — proving\n        the loader-bypass surface is closed at construction time.\"\"\"\n\n        with pytest.raises(InvalidEvalId) as exc_info:\n            HomeIsolation(\n                eval_id=loader_rejected_eval_id,\n                home_root=scratch_root,\n                session_id=\"sess-vector-4\",\n            )\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == loader_rejected_eval_id\n\n    def test_loader_bypass_setup_still_fails_when_post_init_disabled(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        permissive_config: EvalConfig,\n        monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Second-layer defense: even if the constructor's\n        ``__post_init__`` regex check were *removed* by a refactor\n        (simulated by replacing it with a slot-only initializer), the\n        guard inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` MUST still reject the\n        unsafe id with :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`.\n\n        Without this layered defense the loader-bypass risk would\n        re-appear the moment a future refactor weakened the\n        constructor's guarantees. Asserting it here pins\n        defense-in-depth as a *property*, not as a single line of\n        code.\n\n        The patch deliberately targets ``__post_init__`` (not\n        ``validate_eval_id``), so the guard's own\n        :func:`validate_eval_id` reference remains the real\n        implementation — the two layers stay genuinely independent.\n        We use a tamper id (``\"9bad\"``) whose textual form is a legal\n        POSIX filename component so ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` succeeds\n        cleanly under ``scratch_root`` and the failure that surfaces\n        is the guard's eval_id check, not a kernel-level\n        ``mkdtemp`` ``OSError``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        def _slot_only_init(self_inner: HomeIsolation) -> None:\n            \"\"\"Replacement for ``__post_init__`` that initializes the\n            private slot but skips the FR-SCH2 validation.\"\"\"\n            object.__setattr__(self_inner, \"_home_path\", None)\n\n        monkeypatch.setattr(HomeIsolation, \"__post_init__\", _slot_only_init)\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"9bad\",  # FR-SCH2-rejected; safe filename prefix\n            home_root=scratch_root,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-4b\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=permissive_config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"eval_id\"\n        # ``containment_guard`` chains the real :class:`InvalidEvalId`\n        # via ``__cause__`` — proving the second layer is genuinely\n        # independent of the disabled constructor check.\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, InvalidEvalId)\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == \"9bad\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Coverage assertion: every NFR-SEC2 vector is exercised\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_attack_matrix_coverage_is_complete() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Pin the four NFR-SEC2 vectors in code so a future refactor that\n    deletes a vector class is caught here rather than silently shrinking\n    the matrix.\n\n    The roadmap row 30 wording — *\"scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME attack;\n    scratch-outside-allowlist; eval_id mutation post-construction;\n    loader-bypass still rejected\"* — is the canonical four-item list.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    expected = {\n        \"scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME\": TestVectorScratchSymlinkToHome,\n        \"scratch-outside-allowlist\": TestVectorScratchOutsideAllowlist,\n        \"eval_id-mutation-post-construction\": TestVectorEvalIdMutationPostConstruction,\n        \"loader-bypass\": TestVectorLoaderBypass,\n    }\n    assert len(expected) == 4\n    # Every vector class is importable from this module (catches a\n    # rename or accidental deletion).\n    for _vector, cls in expected.items():\n        assert cls.__module__ == __name__\n","numLines":429,"startLine":1,"totalLines":429}}}

The scratch root is a real (non-symlinked) directory whose path is simply absent from the supplied :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. Catches an off-policy ``--scratch-root`` CLI flag value or a misconfigured deployment.\n25\t\n26\tVector 3 (`eval_id-mutation-post-construction`) bypasses the frozen-dataclass guarantee via ``object.__setattr__`` (the same escape hatch the class itself uses for the ``_home_path`` slot). The constructor's ``__post_init__`` validation has already passed, so the only thing standing between a tampered id and :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` is :func:`containment_guard`'s eval_id re-check. Tamper values are restricted to FR-SCH2-rejected ids whose textual form is a legal POSIX filename component — path-separator-bearing tampers (``../escape``, ``E1/x``, ``/etc/passwd``) are covered under vector 4 at construction time where ``__post_init__`` rejects them before any filesystem call.\n27\t\n28\tVector 4 (`loader-bypass`) exists in two layers:\n29\t\n30\t- *Construction-time defense* — ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` re-runs :func:`validate_eval_id` so a caller skipping :class:`SuiteLoader` (programmatic test, future REPL, accidental fixture) hard-fails with :class:`InvalidEvalId` before any filesystem operation becomes reachable.\n31\t- *Second-layer defense* — even if the constructor's check were removed by a future refactor (simulated in the test by replacing ``__post_init__`` with a slot-only initializer), :func:`containment_guard`'s own :func:`validate_eval_id` call inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` still rejects. The test deliberately patches ``__post_init__`` (not :func:`validate_eval_id`) so the two layers remain genuinely independent and the regression-pin is meaningful.\n32\t\n33\t## Public API touched\n34\t\n35\tNone. Why three layered checks, not one consolidated check\n6\t\n7\tEach check defends a distinct attack vector with a distinct underlying primitive:\n8\t\n9\t- **Check 1 (eval_id regex)** defends loader-bypass. The loader (T01.07) validates eval_ids pre- and post-expand, but a programmatic caller can construct `HomeIsolation` directly. The re-validation closes that.\n10\t- **Check 2 (scratch-root allowlist)** defends AC12 policy. The allowlist is a per-run policy concept; `resolve_scratch_root` is the single source of truth and the guard funnels through it.\n11\t- **Check 3 (post-mkdtemp symlink resolution)** defends an attack where the textual prefix passes but a symlink component routes the path outside `scratch_root` at runtime. Only `Path.resolve(strict=True)` catches this — `is_relative_to` on un-resolved paths does not.\n12\t\n13\tConsolidating the checks (e.g., a single regex-validated `resolve_scratch_root` for both `home_path` and `scratch_root`) would lose the distinct `check` identifier on the exception, making forensic bucketing harder.\n14\t\n15\t### 2. Why `config` is required (and not synthesized)\n16\t\n17\tThe initial draft accepted `config: EvalConfig | None = None` in both `containment_guard` and `HomeIsolation.setup`. When `None`, `setup` synthesized `EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(self.home_root, *EvalConfig().allowed_scratch_roots))` — the rationale was \"the upstream caller validated `home_root` via `resolve_scratch_root` at the CLI boundary, so trust it here.\"\n18\t\n19\tThe quality-engineer review correctly flagged this as a bypass: `HomeIsolation.__post_init__` re-validates `eval_id` but does NOT validate `home_root`. A caller constructing `HomeIsolation(eval_id=\"E1\", home_root=Path(\"/home/user/.claude\"), session_id=\"x\")` would pass check 2 trivially because `home_root` is in the synthesized allowlist.\n20\t\n21\tTwo options were considered:\n22\t\n23\t| Option | Adopted | Reason |\n24\t|---|---|---|\n25\t| Validate `home_root` against the default `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots` in `__post_init__` | No | Would block tests using `tmp_path`-derived scratch roots; would require either monkey-patching the default or adding a 5th field to DM-006 (schema change). |\n26\t| Remove the fallback; make `config` required | **Yes** | Aligns with production wiring (orchestrator T03.16 always passes an explicit config); tests now build an `EvalConfig` whose allowlist matches their scratch root, mirroring production. |\n27\t\n28\tThe adopted option makes `config=None` impossible: a missing argument is now a `TypeError` at argument-binding time, before any filesystem operation in `setup` runs. This is the strongest form of \"refusal before side effects.\"\n29\t\n30\t### 3. Ordering: mkdtemp first, guard second\n31\t\n32\tThe guard MUST observe the freshly created HOME because check 3 uses `Path.resolve(strict=True)` which raises `FileNotFoundError` for nonexistent paths. The ordering is pinned by:\n33\t\n34\t- `test_setup_runs_containment_guard_after_mkdtemp` — spies on `containment_guard` and asserts `home_path.exists()` is `True` at call time.\n35\t- `TestSymlinkResolvedContainment.test_raises_when_home_path_not_created` — confirms the failure mode when the guard runs ahead of mkdtemp.\n36\t\n37\tThis ordering also means a check 2 failure (allowlist) happens AFTER mkdtemp ran but BEFORE check 3, leaving the partial HOME on disk. `test_setup_failure_preserves_partial_home` pins this so the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) can find and tag the directory.\n38\t\n39\t### 4. Allowlist source-of-truth\n40\t\n41\t`config.allowed_scratch_roots` is the sole source of truth. No other module embeds a hard-coded copy. The guard verifies this via `test_uses_evalconfig_allowed_scratch_roots_as_source_of_truth`, which narrows the allowlist to a single non-canonical entry and proves the guard follows.\n42\t\n43\t### 5. Exception chaining\n44\t\n45\t`raise ... from exc` is used at every check so `HomeContainmentViolation.__cause__` carries:\n46\t\n47\t- `InvalidEvalId` (with its own `eval_id` field, for forensic recovery of the offending string)\n48\t- `ScratchRootViolation` (with `path`, `resolved`, `allowed` fields)\n49\t- `FileNotFoundError` (raised by `Path.resolve(strict=True)`)\n50\t\n51\tReporters that walk the chain can render the underlying detail without parsing `HomeContainmentViolation.detail`.\n52\t\n53\t## Test scaffolding\n54\t\n55\t### `_materialize_home` helper\n56\t\n57\t`tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py:75` — creates a HOME the same way `HomeIsolation.setup` would, so the `Path.resolve(strict=True)` in check 3 succeeds. Uses plain `mkdir` rather than `tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=...)`; the QA review noted this is slightly less production-realistic, but the integration tests (`TestIntegrationWithHomeIsolationSetup`) exercise the real `mkdtemp` path through `HomeIsolation.setup`, so the helper's simplification is acceptable for direct `containment_guard` calls.\n58\t\n59\t### `permissive_config` fixture (T02.07)\n60\t\n61\t`tests/cli/eval/test_home_isolation_extend.py:62` — `EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(scratch_root,))`. Note `True` and `False` are explicitly tested because `isinstance(True, int)` is `True` and Python would coerce booleans to ints in some contexts; `validate_eval_id` rejects them upfront via `isinstance(value, str)`.\n66\t\n67\t### Symlink-chain test (A→B→outside)\n68\t\n69\t`test_raises_on_symlink_chain_escape` builds a chain `home_chain → intermediate → outside` and asserts `Path.resolve(strict=True)` chases the full chain in one call, surfacing `check='home_path_escape'`. When `None`, `setup` synthesized `EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(self.home_root, *EvalConfig().allowed_scratch_roots))` — the rationale was \"the upstream caller validated `home_root` via `resolve_scratch_root` at the CLI boundary, so trust it here.\"\n\nThe quality-engineer review correctly flagged this as a bypass: `HomeIsolation.__post_init__` re-validates `eval_id` but does NOT validate `home_root`. A caller constructing `HomeIsolation(eval_id=\"E1\", home_root=Path(\"/home/user/.claude\"), session_id=\"x\")` would pass check 2 trivially because `home_root` is in the synthesized allowlist.\n\nTwo options were considered:\n\n| Option | Adopted | Reason |\n|---|---|---|\n| Validate `home_root` against the default `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots` in `__post_init__` | No | Would block tests using `tmp_path`-derived scratch roots; would require either monkey-patching the default or adding a 5th field to DM-006 (schema change). |\n| Remove the fallback; make `config` required | **Yes** | Aligns with production wiring (orchestrator T03.16 always passes an explicit config); tests now build an `EvalConfig` whose allowlist matches their scratch root, mirroring production. |\n\nThe adopted option makes `config=None` impossible: a missing argument is now a `TypeError` at argument-binding time, before any filesystem operation in `setup` runs. This is the strongest form of \"refusal before side effects.\"\n\n### 3. The ordering is pinned by:\n\n- `test_setup_runs_containment_guard_after_mkdtemp` — spies on `containment_guard` and asserts `home_path.exists()` is `True` at call time.\n- `TestSymlinkResolvedContainment.test_raises_when_home_path_not_created` — confirms the failure mode when the guard runs ahead of mkdtemp.\n\nThis ordering also means a check 2 failure (allowlist) happens AFTER mkdtemp ran but BEFORE check 3, leaving the partial HOME on disk. `test_setup_failure_preserves_partial_home` pins this so the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) can find and tag the directory.\n\n### 4. The guard verifies this via `test_uses_evalconfig_allowed_scratch_roots_as_source_of_truth`, which narrows the allowlist to a single non-canonical entry and proves the guard follows.\n\n### 5. Exception chaining\n\n`raise ... from exc` is used at every check so `HomeContainmentViolation.__cause__` carries:\n\n- `InvalidEvalId` (with its own `eval_id` field, for forensic recovery of the offending string)\n- `ScratchRootViolation` (with `path`, `resolved`, `allowed` fields)\n- `FileNotFoundError` (raised by `Path.resolve(strict=True)`)\n\nReporters that walk the chain can render the underlying detail without parsing `HomeContainmentViolation.detail`.\n\n## Test scaffolding\n\n### `_materialize_home` helper\n\n`tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py:75` — creates a HOME the same way `HomeIsolation.setup` would, so the `Path.resolve(strict=True)` in check 3 succeeds. The 29 `iso.setup(config=permissive_config)` callsites in T02.07 now mirror production wiring: the orchestrator (T03.16) builds an `EvalConfig` whose allowlist contains the resolved scratch root for the run.\n\n### Non-string eval_id parametrize sweep\n\n`test_non_string_eval_id_is_rejected` covers `42, 0, -1, True, False, None, Path(\"E1\"), b\"E1\", [\"E1\"], {\"id\": \"E1\"}, (\"E1\",), 3.14`. Note `True` and `False` are explicitly tested because `isinstance(True, int)` is `True` and Python would coerce booleans to ints in some contexts; `validate_eval_id` rejects them upfront via `isinstance(value, str)`.\n\n### Symlink-chain test (A→B→outside)\n\n`test_raises_on_symlink_chain_escape` builds a chain `home_chain → intermediate → outside` and asserts `Path.resolve(strict=True)` chases the full chain in one call, surfacing `check='home_path_escape'`. The\n        canonical ``/tmp/eval-runs`` entry alone must let the call\n        through.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        config = EvalConfig()  # canonical M1 defaults — no override\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",\n            home_root=tmp_eval_runs_subdir,\n            session_id=\"sess-tmp-slice1\",\n        )\n\n        home = iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        # The per-eval HOME was minted under the canonical allowlist\n        # entry — proving the default-config allowlist is the\n        # acceptance surface.\n        assert home.resolve().is_relative_to(_TMP_EVAL_RUNS_ROOT.resolve())\n        assert iso.is_set_up is True\n\n        iso.teardown(keep=False)\n\n    def test_dev_eval_runs_accepted_under_default_config(\n        self, dev_eval_runs_subdir: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Repo ``.dev/eval-runs`` is accepted by the default config.\n\n        ``.dev/eval-runs`` ships *relative* in the default allowlist\n        so resolution anchors against the process CWD.
