The chained\n    ``__cause__`` is the :class:`ScratchRootViolation` from\n    :func:`resolve_scratch_root` — the single AC12 source of truth.\n\n    Both ingress surfaces are exercised:\n\n    * :func:`containment_guard` called directly (the unit boundary).\n    * :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` (the integration boundary).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\n        \"rejected_subpath\",\n        [\n            \"user-home-dot-claude\",  # stands in for ``$HOME/.claude``\n            \"etc-style-system-path\",  # stands in for ``/etc/...``\n            \"var-lib-eval-runs\",      # ``/var/lib`` close-but-not-allowed\n            \"root-dot-claude\",        # stands in for ``/root/.claude``\n            \"tmp-other-runs\",         # ``/tmp/other-runs`` prefix-collision\n        ],\n    )\n    def test_setup_rejects_root_outside_default_allowlist(\n        self, tmp_path: Path, rejected_subpath: str\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"A ``tmp_path``-derived directory is the safest stand-in for\n        every \"non-allowlisted\" filesystem location an operator might\n        mis-supply. The default :class:`EvalConfig` allowlist does NOT\n        cover ``tmp_path``, so every parametrized scratch_root must\n        fail with ``check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        scratch = tmp_path / rejected_subpath\n        scratch.mkdir(parents=True)\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",\n            home_root=scratch,\n            session_id=\"sess-rejected\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=EvalConfig())  # default allowlist — rejects\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, ScratchRootViolation)\n        # The verbatim (un-resolved) ``scratch_root`` is preserved for\n        # forensic rendering — operators see exactly what they typed.\n        assert exc_info.value.scratch_root == scratch\n\n    def test_containment_guard_rejects_root_outside_default_allowlist(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Unit-level mirror: pure ``containment_guard`` rejects the\n        same way :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` does. Keeps the guard\n        from drifting away from the setup wrapper's behavior.\"\"\"\n\n        scratch = tmp_path / \"off-policy-scratch\"\n        scratch.mkdir()\n        home = scratch / \"E1-home\"\n        home.mkdir()\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            containment_guard(\n                home_path=home,\n                scratch_root=scratch,\n                eval_id=\"E1\",\n                config=EvalConfig(),\n            )\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, ScratchRootViolation)\n\n    def test_narrowed_allowlist_rejects_canonical_tmp_eval_runs(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"The allowlist is the *sole* source of truth: a config that\n        narrows the allowlist to a single non-canonical entry must\n        reject even the canonical ``/tmp/eval-runs`` prefix. Proves\n        :class:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots` is what drives the\n        check — nothing inside :func:`containment_guard` synthesizes\n        a canonical fallback after the fact (the NFR-SEC2 bypass\n        closure).\"\"\"\n\n        only_allowed = tmp_path / \"narrowed-allowlist-only\"\n        only_allowed.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(only_allowed,))\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",\n            home_root=_TMP_EVAL_RUNS_ROOT,\n            session_id=\"sess-narrowed\",\n        )\n\n        # The canonical root is NOT in the narrowed allowlist — must reject.\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Slice 3 — loader-bypass defense (TEST-002 AC)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestLoaderBypassDefense:\n    \"\"\"Slice 3 of TEST-002.\n\n    NFR-SEC2 requires that a caller constructing :class:`HomeIsolation`\n    directly — bypassing :class:`SuiteLoader` — still hard-fails\n    containment. Two independent layers enforce the property:\n\n    * Layer A: ``__post_init__`` re-runs :func:`validate_eval_id` so\n      an unsafe ``eval_id`` raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` at\n      construction, before any filesystem write becomes reachable.\n    * Layer B: :func:`containment_guard` re-runs the same check\n      inside :meth:`setup`, so a future refactor that weakens\n      ``__post_init__`` does not re-open the bypass.\n\n    Both layers are pinned here. The acceptance criterion specifically\n    calls out \"constructing HomeIsolation directly (without SuiteLoader)\n    and confirms containment still applies\" — layer A is the most\n    direct readout of that, and layer B keeps the defense layered.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\n        \"loader_rejected_eval_id\",\n        [\n            \"../escape\",         # path traversal\n            \"/etc/passwd\",       # absolute-path smuggling\n            \"E1/with/sep\",       # embedded path separator\n            \"..\",                # parent-dir literal\n            \"9bad\",              # FR-SCH2 leading-digit reject\n            \"\",                  # empty string\n            \"with spaces\",       # whitespace\n            \"{{template}}\",      # un-expanded parameterize token\n            \"${shell}\",          # shell-substitution smuggling\n            \"E1\\nE2\",            # newline injection\n        ],\n    )\n    def test_direct_construction_rejects_loader_rejected_id(\n        self,\n        tmp_path: Path,\n        loader_rejected_eval_id: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Layer A: a caller skipping :class:`SuiteLoader` and feeding\n        an unsafe id to :class:`HomeIsolation` hard-fails at\n        construction time (``__post_init__``).\"\"\"\n\n        # ``home_root`` is intentionally a real, in-allowlist tmp_path —\n        # we want the failure surface to be the eval_id check, not a\n        # coincidental allowlist miss.\n        with pytest.raises(InvalidEvalId) as exc_info:\n            HomeIsolation(\n                eval_id=loader_rejected_eval_id,\n                home_root=tmp_path,\n                session_id=\"sess-loader-bypass-A\",\n            )\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == loader_rejected_eval_id\n\n    def test_direct_construction_with_post_init_disabled_is_caught_by_guard(\n        self,\n        tmp_path: Path,\n        monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Layer B: even if ``__post_init__`` is replaced with a\n        slot-only initializer (simulating a refactor that drops the\n        constructor check), :func:`containment_guard` inside\n        :meth:`setup` MUST still reject the unsafe id.\n\n        Patches ``__post_init__`` rather than :func:`validate_eval_id`\n        so the guard's own validator reference remains the real\n        implementation — the two layers must stay independent.\n        Uses ``\"9bad\"`` (FR-SCH2-rejected; legal POSIX filename) so\n        ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` succeeds and the failure surfaces from\n        the guard, not from the kernel.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        scratch = tmp_path / \"scratch\"\n        scratch.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(scratch,))\n\n        def _slot_only_init(self_inner: HomeIsolation) -> None:\n            object.__setattr__(self_inner, \"_home_path\", None)\n\n        monkeypatch.setattr(HomeIsolation, \"__post_init__\", _slot_only_init)\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"9bad\",  # FR-SCH2 reject; legal filename\n            home_root=scratch,\n            session_id=\"sess-loader-bypass-B\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"eval_id\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, InvalidEvalId)\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == \"9bad\"\n\n    def test_direct_construction_without_loader_still_hits_allowlist_check(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"A loader-bypassing caller that supplies a valid id but a\n        non-allowlisted ``home_root`` ALSO hits containment — pins\n        that the bypass closure covers both the eval_id check and\n        the scratch-root check (loader bypass is not \"eval_id-only\").\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Default allowlist does not include ``tmp_path`` — perfect\n        # stand-in for \"any non-allowlisted location an attacker could\n        # smuggle past the loader\".\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",\n            home_root=tmp_path,\n            session_id=\"sess-loader-bypass-C\",\n        )\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=EvalConfig())  # canonical defaults\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, ScratchRootViolation)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Slice 4 — exit-code-2 path covered (TEST-002 AC)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestExitCodeTwoPath:\n    \"\"\"Slice 4 of TEST-002.\n\n    Every containment-failure exception that reaches the CLI boundary\n    MUST map to exit code ``2``. There are two underlying causes a\n    :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` can chain (per FR-ISO2):\n\n    * :class:`InvalidEvalId` — CLI mapping\n      :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    * :class:`ScratchRootViolation` — CLI mapping\n      :data:`SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n    The constants are asserted as ``== 2`` so a future drift of the\n    exit-code mapping breaks this test before any operator sees a\n    wrong exit code. ``home_root`` is intentionally a real, in-allowlist tmp_path —\n        # we want the failure surface to be the eval_id check, not a\n        # coincidental allowlist miss.\n        with pytest.raises(InvalidEvalId) as exc_info:\n            HomeIsolation(\n                eval_id=loader_rejected_eval_id,\n                home_root=tmp_path,\n                session_id=\"sess-loader-bypass-A\",\n            )\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == loader_rejected_eval_id\n\n    def test_direct_construction_with_post_init_disabled_is_caught_by_guard(\n        self,\n        tmp_path: Path,\n        monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Layer B: even if ``__post_init__`` is replaced with a\n        slot-only initializer (simulating a refactor that drops the\n        constructor check), :func:`containment_guard` inside\n        :meth:`setup` MUST still reject the unsafe id.\n\n        Patches ``__post_init__`` rather than :func:`validate_eval_id`\n        so the guard's own validator reference remains the real\n        implementation — the two layers must stay independent.\n        Uses ``\"9bad\"`` (FR-SCH2-rejected; legal POSIX filename) so\n        ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` succeeds and the failure surfaces from\n        the guard, not from the kernel.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        scratch = tmp_path / \"scratch\"\n        scratch.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(scratch,))\n\n        def _slot_only_init(self_inner: HomeIsolation) -> None:\n            object.__setattr__(self_inner, \"_home_path\", None)\n\n        monkeypatch.setattr(HomeIsolation, \"__post_init__\", _slot_only_init)\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"9bad\",  # {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01DXBqVDgZSEL5eQziryedVs","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01L9BM5B5rr8cw8v39aDAvjD","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0040/spec.md","content":"# D-0040 — TEST-002 containment unit tests\n\n**Task**: T02.21 (Phase 2 — cliEval harness)\n**Tier**: STRICT\n**Risk**: High (security)\n**Roadmap**: R-040 / TEST-002 (allowed roots accepted, non-allowlisted roots rejected, loader-bypass defense, exit-code-2 path)\n**Cross-links**: D-0028 (HomeIsolation surface, T02.07), D-0029 (FR-ISO2 path containment guard, T02.08), D-0030 (NFR-SEC2 defense-in-depth, T02.09), AC12 / T01.19 (`resolve_scratch_root`), FR-SCH2 / T01.05 (`validate_eval_id`)\n\n## Goal\n\nTEST-002 is the **contract-layer readout** for the M2 exit checkpoint. The sibling deliverables (D-0029 unit, D-0030 attack matrix) prove the three checks of `containment_guard` and the four NFR-SEC2 attack vectors. D-0040 is the *first-class TEST item* the roadmap promises: one targeted pytest module, named after the boundary it pins (`test_containment.py`), whose every test maps directly to a TEST-002 acceptance-criterion bullet, so that the audit reviewing the M2 exit can read the file top-to-bottom and confirm the contract without spelunking through three sibling modules.\n\nThe module exercises **real canonical scratch roots** (`/tmp/eval-runs`, `.dev/eval-runs`) under the **default** `EvalConfig()` allowlist — not a narrowed test config — so the test would fail if either canonical root were ever removed from `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. It also pins both exit-code constants (`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`, `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE`) as `== 2` literals, so any drift on the exit-code contract surfaces here.\n\n## Test matrix\n\n| Slice | TEST-002 AC bullet | Test class | Cases |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Allowed roots accepted | `repo .dev accepted`, `/tmp accepted` | `TestAllowedRootsAccepted` | 4 |\n| Non-allowlisted roots rejected | `non-allowlisted root rejected` | `TestNonAllowlistedRootsRejected` | 7 (5 parametrized + 2) |\n| Loader-bypass defense | `loader-bypass rejected` | `TestLoaderBypassDefense` | 12 (10 parametrized + 2) |\n| Exit-code-2 path | `exit-2 path covered` | `TestExitCodeTwoPath` | 6 |\n| Coverage pin | (meta) | `test_test_002_slice_coverage_is_complete` | 1 |\n| **Total** | | | **30** |\n\n### Slice 1 — `TestAllowedRootsAccepted` (4 cases)\n\n- `test_tmp_eval_runs_accepted_under_default_config` — `HomeIsolation.setup(config=EvalConfig())` succeeds with a `/tmp/eval-runs/<unique>` subdir (real path created and torn down).\n- `test_dev_eval_runs_accepted_under_default_config` — same, for `.dev/eval-runs/<unique>` (repo-anchored canonical root).\n- `test_containment_guard_passes_for_tmp_eval_runs` — `containment_guard(...)` returns cleanly for an allowed root + resolved tempdir.\n- `test_containment_guard_passes_for_dev_eval_runs` — same, for the repo-anchored canonical root.\n\n### Slice 2 — `TestNonAllowlistedRootsRejected` (7 cases)\n\n- `test_setup_rejects_root_outside_default_allowlist` (parametrized × 5): `~/.claude`-style user-home path, `/etc`-style system path, `/var/lib/eval-runs`, `/root/.claude`, `/tmp/other-runs`. Each must raise `HomeContainmentViolation(check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\")` with `ScratchRootViolation` as `__cause__`.\n- `test_containment_guard_rejects_root_outside_default_allowlist` — unit-level rejection via direct `containment_guard(...)` call against a non-allowlisted `tmp_path`.\n- `test_narrowed_allowlist_rejects_canonical_tmp_eval_runs` — *inverse* assertion: when a caller narrows the allowlist (e.g. to repo `.dev` only), the canonical `/tmp/eval-runs` is rejected. Proves the allowlist is a *single source of truth* — not a hardcoded constant inside `containment_guard`.\n\n### Slice 3 — `TestLoaderBypassDefense` (12 cases)\n\nThis is the highest-stakes slice — TEST-002 explicitly requires direct construction of `HomeIsolation` without `SuiteLoader` to confirm containment still applies.\n\n- `test_direct_construction_rejects_loader_rejected_id` (parametrized × 10): for each FR-SCH2-rejected id (`../escape`, `/etc/passwd`, `E1/with/sep`, `..`, `9bad`, empty string, `with spaces`, `{{template}}`, `${shell}`, `E1\\nE2`), constructing `HomeIsolation(eval_id=<bad>, ...)` directly (not through `SuiteLoader`) raises `InvalidEvalId` from `__post_init__` before any filesystem call.\n- `test_direct_construction_with_post_init_disabled_is_caught_by_guard` — simulates a future refactor that removes the constructor check by monkeypatching `__post_init__` to a slot-only initializer; confirms `containment_guard`'s second-layer `validate_eval_id` call still rejects. Patches `__post_init__` (not `validate_eval_id`) so the two layers remain genuinely independent.\n- `test_direct_construction_without_loader_still_hits_allowlist_check` — direct (non-loader) construction with a *valid* `eval_id` but a non-allowlisted scratch root: `HomeContainmentViolation(check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\")` still fires, proving the allowlist check is not coupled to loader-mediated entry.\n\n### Slice 4 — `TestExitCodeTwoPath` (6 cases)\n\n- `test_invalid_eval_id_exit_code_is_two` — `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE == 2` (literal pin).\n- `test_scratch_root_violation_exit_code_is_two` — `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2` (literal pin).\n- `test_exit_codes_are_aligned_with_each_other` — the two constants are equal (so CLI exit-code policy treats both rejection paths uniformly).\n- `test_containment_failure_chains_exit2_cause_for_eval_id` — end-to-end: a real containment failure on a tampered `eval_id` chains `InvalidEvalId` as `__cause__`, and `InvalidEvalId`'s exit-code constant is 2.\n- `test_containment_failure_chains_exit2_cause_for_scratch_root` — same, for a real containment failure on a non-allowlisted scratch root, chaining `ScratchRootViolation`.\n- `test_direct_loader_bypass_exit_path_is_invalid_eval_id` — direct construction with a bad id raises `InvalidEvalId` whose exit-code is 2.\n\n### Coverage pin — `test_test_002_slice_coverage_is_complete`\n\nA meta-test that asserts each TEST-002 AC bullet has at least one corresponding test class in this module. Future drift in the AC list will break this test, forcing a coordinated spec + test update.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n| AC | Source | Verified by |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC1 — repo `.dev` accepted | TEST-002 | `TestAllowedRootsAccepted::test_dev_eval_runs_accepted_under_default_config`, `…::test_containment_guard_passes_for_dev_eval_runs` |\n| AC2 — `/tmp` accepted | TEST-002 | `TestAllowedRootsAccepted::test_tmp_eval_runs_accepted_under_default_config`, `…::test_containment_guard_passes_for_tmp_eval_runs` |\n| AC3 — non-allowlisted root rejected | TEST-002 | `TestNonAllowlistedRootsRejected` (all 7 cases) |\n| AC4 — loader-bypass rejected | TEST-002 | `TestLoaderBypassDefense` (all 12 cases) |\n| AC5 — exit-2 path covered | TEST-002 | `TestExitCodeTwoPath` (all 6 cases) |\n| AC6 — pytest exits 0 | TEST-002 | Evidence log: 30 passed in 0.16s |\n| AC7 — direct construction without `SuiteLoader` still contained | TEST-002 | `TestLoaderBypassDefense::test_direct_construction_without_loader_still_hits_allowlist_check`, `…::test_direct_construction_with_post_init_disabled_is_caught_by_guard` |\n| AC8 — `D-0040/spec.md` documents the test matrix | TEST-002 | this document |\n\n## Why a new module instead of extending an existing one\n\n| Existing module | Purpose | Why it can't be TEST-002 |\n|---|---|---|\n| `test_path_containment.py` | FR-ISO2 unit — three checks of `containment_guard` in isolation | Asserts *internal* guard behavior; doesn't pin `EvalConfig` defaults or exit-code contract |\n| `test_defense_in_depth.py` (D-0030) | NFR-SEC2 attack matrix — four named vectors | Vector-oriented framing, not AC-oriented; loader-bypass slice is one vector among four |\n| `test_scratch_root_allowlist.py` | AC12 unit — `resolve_scratch_root` standalone | Scoped to the allowlist resolver; doesn't cover `eval_id` or exit-codes |\n\nTEST-002 is the *contract* — one module whose every test maps to an AC bullet, designed to be read by the M2-exit auditor. Folding it into any existing module would dilute the readout and re-introduce the same scattered-coverage problem the TEST-002 deliverable was created to fix.\n\n## Public API touched\n\nNone. D-0040 is a test-only deliverable. Production surface was finalized by D-0028 / D-0029 / D-0030. The exit-code constants and the `default` `EvalConfig()` allowlist are now *load-bearing* for this module — any future change to either will surface here first.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1617,"cache_read_input_tokens":76378,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1617,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"393dbbea-2f8b-4d05-a446-6e0c9a43ecd9","uuid":"8dada930-ef33-406a-af64-afadbfec5f48"}

Future drift in the AC list will break this test, forcing a coordinated spec + test update.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n| AC | Source | Verified by |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC1 — repo `.dev` accepted | TEST-002 | `TestAllowedRootsAccepted::test_dev_eval_runs_accepted_under_default_config`, `…::test_containment_guard_passes_for_dev_eval_runs` |\n| AC2 — `/tmp` accepted | TEST-002 | `TestAllowedRootsAccepted::test_tmp_eval_runs_accepted_under_default_config`, `…::test_containment_guard_passes_for_tmp_eval_runs` |\n| AC3 — non-allowlisted root rejected | TEST-002 | `TestNonAllowlistedRootsRejected` (all 7 cases) |\n| AC4 — loader-bypass rejected | TEST-002 | `TestLoaderBypassDefense` (all 12 cases) |\n| AC5 — exit-2 path covered | TEST-002 | `TestExitCodeTwoPath` (all 6 cases) |\n| AC6 — pytest exits 0 | TEST-002 | Evidence log: 30 passed in 0.16s |\n| AC7 — direct construction without `SuiteLoader` still contained | TEST-002 | `TestLoaderBypassDefense::test_direct_construction_without_loader_still_hits_allowlist_check`, `…::test_direct_construction_with_post_init_disabled_is_caught_by_guard` |\n| AC8 — `D-0040/spec.md` documents the test matrix | TEST-002 | this document |\n\n## Why a new module instead of extending an existing one\n\n| Existing module | Purpose | Why it can't be TEST-002 |\n|---|---|---|\n| `test_path_containment.py` | FR-ISO2 unit — three checks of `containment_guard` in isolation | Asserts *internal* guard behavior; doesn't pin `EvalConfig` defaults or exit-code contract |\n| `test_defense_in_depth.py` (D-0030) | NFR-SEC2 attack matrix — four named vectors | Vector-oriented framing, not AC-oriented; loader-bypass slice is one vector among four |\n| `test_scratch_root_allowlist.py` | AC12 unit — `resolve_scratch_root` standalone | Scoped to the allowlist resolver; doesn't cover `eval_id` or exit-codes |\n\nTEST-002 is the *contract* — one module whose every test maps to an AC bullet, designed to be read by the M2-exit auditor. Folding it into any existing module would dilute the readout and re-introduce the same scattered-coverage problem the TEST-002 deliverable was created to fix.\n\n## Public API touched\n\nNone. D-0040 is a test-only deliverable. Production surface was finalized by D-0028 / D-0029 / D-0030. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01LKhEKMwRUQt6dxzBPQYpB5","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Ei7bhhqjWV6yjXs99X1xYc","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0040/notes.md","content":"# D-0040 — Design notes\n\n## Why use real canonical roots instead of a narrowed test config\n\nThe natural way to write these tests is to construct an `EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(tmp_path,))` and point the scratch root at that tempdir — clean, hermetic, no filesystem pollution. **D-0040 deliberately does not do this.**\n\nThe TEST-002 AC bullets cite `/tmp` and the repo `.dev` directory *by name*. The contract being pinned is not \"the allowlist works\" (D-0029 already pins that). The contract is **\"the default `EvalConfig()` allowlist contains exactly these two canonical roots.\"** If a future refactor accidentally removed `/tmp/eval-runs` from the default, every test in this repo using a narrowed allowlist would still pass — but the harness would no longer match the operator-facing documentation that says \"drop your eval there.\" D-0040 binds the test to the default config so that drift surfaces immediately.\n\nThe cost is a `tmp_eval_runs_subdir` fixture that creates a real `/tmp/eval-runs/test-containment-<uuid>` directory and tears it down in `finally:`. The `<uuid>` suffix prevents collision with concurrent test workers and live eval runs. Cleanup uses `shutil.rmtree(..., ignore_errors=True)` so a leaked HomeIsolation handle inside a failed test doesn't break the fixture teardown.\n\n## Why pin exit codes as `== 2` literals\n\n`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` and `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` are exported constants — the obvious test is `assert INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE == SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE`. That asserts *consistency* but not *value*. The operator-facing contract is \"rejections from the containment system exit with code 2\"; if both constants drifted to `3` together, the consistency check would still pass but the contract would silently break. D-0040 includes three exit-code assertions:\n\n1. `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE == 2` (literal pin)\n2. `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2` (literal pin)\n3. The two are aligned with each other (consistency pin)\n\nAll three must hold simultaneously. Any single one being false catches a different class of regression.\n\n## Why patch `__post_init__` instead of `validate_eval_id` for the loader-bypass second-layer test\n\nThe second-layer test (`test_direct_construction_with_post_init_disabled_is_caught_by_guard`) needs to prove `containment_guard` still catches a bad `eval_id` even when the constructor's check has been bypassed. The naïve approach is `monkeypatch.setattr(loader, \"validate_eval_id\", lambda *_: None)`. **D-0040 does not do this** — that would patch the function *both* in `__post_init__` *and* in `containment_guard`, so the test would prove nothing about layered defense (both layers would be disabled simultaneously).\n\nInstead, D-0040 patches `HomeIsolation.__post_init__` to a slot-only initializer (assigning only `_home_path` via `object.__setattr__`). This disables *only* the constructor check; `containment_guard`'s own `validate_eval_id` call inside `setup()` still runs unmodified. The test then confirms the guard catches the bad id at `setup()` time. The two defense layers remain genuinely independent.\n\n## Why parametrize FR-SCH2-rejected ids in the loader-bypass slice\n\nTen parametrized cases (`../escape`, `/etc/passwd`, `E1/with/sep`, `..`, `9bad`, ``, `with spaces`, `{{template}}`, `${shell}`, `E1\\nE2`) cover the FR-SCH2 rejection regex from multiple angles:\n- **Path separators**: `../escape`, `/etc/passwd`, `E1/with/sep`, `..` — would escape the tempdir if mkdtemp ran\n- **Lowercase first char**: `9bad` — violates the `^[A-Z]` anchor\n- **Empty / whitespace**: ``, `with spaces` — bypasses tokenization\n- **Template / shell metacharacters**: `{{template}}`, `${shell}` — defends against injection if the id ever reaches a shell\n- **Control characters**: `E1\\nE2` — defends against log/file-format injection\n\nEach is a single AC case (`AC4 — loader-bypass rejected`), but the parametrization shape means a future regex relaxation that allowed, say, lowercase first chars would break exactly one parametrized case, making the regression obvious.\n\n## Why a coverage-pin meta-test\n\n`test_test_002_slice_coverage_is_complete` walks the AC bullet list and asserts each has a corresponding test class in the module. This is overkill for a 4-class module — but the same pattern is used in D-0030 (5 vector classes), and uniformity matters more than minimal cost. The cost is one assertion; the value is forcing future AC additions to land with their tests in the same PR.\n\n## Sub-agent review checkpoint\n\nT02.21 is STRICT tier with `Sub-Agent Delegation: Required` (quality-engineer). Per phase-2-tasklist: \"Run `uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_containment.py -v` + sub-agent review.\" The pytest run is in `evidence.md`; sub-agent review note attached there.\n\n## Sibling regression\n\nAfter landing this module, the four-module containment family (`test_containment.py` + `test_defense_in_depth.py` + `test_path_containment.py` + `test_scratch_root_allowlist.py`) runs clean at **115 passed in 0.26s**.
